=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given a string S of length N consisting of digits from 1 through 9.
For each pair of integers (i,j) \ (1\leq i\leq j\leq N), define f(i, j) as the value obtained by interpreting the substring of S from the i-th through the j-th character as a decimal integer. Find \displaystyle \sum_{i=1}^N \sum_{j=i}^N f(i, j).

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N
S

Output

Print the answer.

Constraints


- 1 \leq N \leq 2 \times 10^5
- N is an integer.
- S is a string of length N consisting of digits from 1 through 9.

Sample Input 1

3
379

Sample Output 1

514

The answer is f(1,1) + f(1,2) + f(1,3) + f(2,2) + f(2,3) + f(3,3) = 3 + 37 + 379 + 7 + 79 + 9 = 514.

Sample Input 2

30
314159265358979323846264338327

Sample Output 2

369673254065355789035427227741

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python

```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Pseudo:

import sys

def main():
    input = sys.stdin.readline
    N = int(input())
    S = input().strip()
    digits = [0]  # digit at 10^0 of A
    carry = 0
    B = 0

        rem = cur % 9

        start += 1

Need ensure formula A = sum c_t * 10^{N-t+1}. Let's rederive from contribution formula to avoid off-by-one.

Original answer = sum_{i=1}^N sum_{j=i}^N value S[i..j].
For each position k (1-indexed), digit d_k appears in substrings starting i<=k and ending j>=k. Its place value in substring i..j is 10^{j-k}. For fixed k, sum over j=k..N of 10^{j-k} = 1 + 10 + ... + 10^{N-k} = (10^{N-k+1}-1)/9. Number of starts i=1..k is k. Contribution = d_k * k * (10^{N-k+1}-1)/9.
Let c_k = d_k*k. Answer = sum c_k * (10^{N-k+1}-1)/9 = (sum c_k 10^{N-k+1} - sum c_k)/9. Yes A exponent e=N-k+1. For k=N, e=1; k=1, e=N. Good.

Thus digits generation e=1..N with c_N at e=1, c_{N-1} at e=2, etc. We iterate t=N..1 and append digit for e. But our loop appends digit after adding c_t. Let's verify with e=1: carry initially 0, add c_N, append carry%10. But what about units digit e=0? We prepend 0. Good. But wait A has no 10^0 term, so units digit 0. However, when adding c_N at e=1, if c_N has a units digit, it contributes to 10^1 digit? Actually c_N * 10^1: c_N's units digit goes to 10^1, c_N's tens digit goes to 10^2, etc. So at e=1, digit = c_N % 10, carry = c_N //10. Our code: carry += c_N (27), append carry%10=7, carry//=10=2. Yes.

At e=2, carry from previous (2) represents c_N's tens digit contribution to 10^2. Add c_{N-1} (14), total 16, append digit 6, carry=1. This represents c_N hundreds? Wait c_N=27: 27*10 = 270 = 7*10 + 2*100. At e=2, carry 2 from c_N tens plus c_{N-1} units 4 => 6 at 10^2, carry 1 to 10^3. Good.

Implementation:

import sys

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()
    if not data: return
    N = int(data[0])
    S = data[1].strip()
    digits = [0]
    carry = 0
    B = 0
    # local variables for speed
    for t in range(N, 0, -1):
        c = (S[t-1] - 48) * t
        B += c
        carry += c
        digits.append(carry % 10)
        carry //= 10
    while carry:
        digits.append(carry % 10)
        carry //= 10

    # subtract B
    borrow = 0
    b = B
    i = 0
    while b > 0 or borrow:
        if i == len(digits):
            digits.append(0)  # should not happen
        sub = b % 10 if b else 0
        if b:
            b //= 10
        val = digits[i] - sub - borrow
        if val < 0:
            val += 10
            borrow = 1
        else:
            borrow = 0
        digits[i] = val
        i += 1

    # strip leading zeros
    while len(digits) > 1 and digits[-1] == 0:
        digits.pop()

    # divide by 9
    q = []
    rem = 0
    # local append
    append = q.append
    for d in reversed(digits):
        cur = rem * 10 + d
        append(cur // 9)
        rem = cur % 9
    # rem should be 0
    start = 0
    while start < len(q) - 1 and q[start] == 0:
        start += 1
    sys.stdout.write(''.join(map(str, q[start:])))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

Code:

import sys

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()
    if not data:
        return
    N = int(data[0])
    S = data[1]

    digits = [0]
    carry = 0
    B = 0

    for t in range(N, 0, -1):
        c = (S[t - 1] - 48) * t
        B += c
        carry += c
        digits.append(carry % 10)
        carry //= 10

    while carry:
        digits.append(carry % 10)
        carry //= 10

    # subtract B from A
    borrow = 0
    b = B
    i = 0
    while b > 0 or borrow:
        if i == len(digits):
            digits.append(0)
        sub = b % 10 if b else 0
        if b:
            b //= 10
        val = digits[i] - sub - borrow
        if val < 0:
            val += 10
            borrow = 1
        else:
            borrow = 0
        digits[i] = val
        i += 1

    while len(digits) > 1 and digits[-1] == 0:
        digits.pop()

    out = bytearray()
    rem = 0
    for d in reversed(digits):
        cur = rem * 10 + d
        out.append(48 + cur // 9)
        rem = cur % 9

    start = 0
    while start < len(out) - 1 and out[start] == 48:
        start += 1

    sys.stdout.buffer.write(out[start:])
    sys.stdout.buffer.write(b'\n')

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

    if i == len(digits):

        borrow = 1
    else:
        borrow = 0
    digits[i] = val
    i += 1

At each step e, we add c_t to carry, then output carry%10 as digit at position e, and pass carry//10 to position e+1.
```
import sys

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()
    if not data:
        return
    N = int(data[0])
    S = data[1]

    digits = bytearray([0])
    carry = 0
    B = 0

    for t in range(N, 0, -1):
        c = (S[t - 1] - 48) * t
        B += c
        carry += c
        digits.append(carry % 10)
        carry //= 10

    while carry:
        digits.append(carry % 10)
        carry //= 10

    borrow = 0
    b = B
    i = 0
    while b > 0 or borrow:
        if i == len(digits):
            digits.append(0)
        sub = b % 10
        b //= 10
        val = digits[i] - sub - borrow
        if val < 0:
            val += 10
            borrow = 1
        else:
            borrow = 0
        digits[i] = val
        i += 1

    while len(digits) > 1 and digits[-1] == 0:
        digits.pop()

    out = bytearray()
    rem = 0
    for d in reversed(digits):
        cur = rem * 10 + d
        out.append(48 + cur // 9)
        rem = cur % 9

    start = 0
    while start < len(out) - 1 and out[start] == 48:
        start += 1

    sys.stdout.buffer.write(out[start:])
    sys.stdout.buffer.write(b'\n')

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with the code if `out` bytearray and `out.append(48 + cur // 9)` where `cur // 9` is int but if `cur` is 89, append 57. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with the code if `rem` after long division is 0 but we don't check; okay.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is any issue with the code if `data` has S as bytes and `S[t - 1] - 48` for t=N down to1; if S is a `bytes` object, indexing returns int. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with the code if `N` is 200000 and `range(N,0,-1)` is large but okay.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with the code if `digits` bytearray and `digits.append(carry % 10)` where `carry % 10` is int but if `carry` is a Python int and `carry % 10` returns int; okay.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with the code if `out` bytearray and `out[start:]` if start is 0 and out is bytearray; write all. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with the code if `sys.stdout.buffer.write(out[start:])` and then `sys.stdout.buffer.write(b'\n')`; if output is huge, two writes okay. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with the code if `data` is empty and we return; no output. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with the code if `N` is 1 and S="1": output "1\n". Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with the code if `N` is 1 and S="9": output "9\n". Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with the code if `N` is 2 and S="12": output "15\n". Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with the code if `N` is 2 and S="99": output "117\n". Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with the code if `N` is 3 and S="379": output "514\n". Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with the code if `N` is 3 and S="999": output "1224\n". Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with the code if `N` is 4 and S="1234": output "1670\n". Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with the code if `N` is 4 and S="1000": output "1111\n". Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with the code if `N` is 4 and S="9999": output "12330\n". Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with the code if `
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()
    if not data:
        return

    N = int(data[0])
    S = data[1]

    # digits will store the decimal digits of A = sum c_t * 10^(N-t+1),
    # from least significant to most significant.
    digits = bytearray([0])  # 10^0 digit of A is always 0
    carry = 0
    B = 0  # B = sum c_t

    for t in range(N, 0, -1):
        c = (S[t - 1] - 48) * t
        B += c
        carry += c
        digits.append(carry % 10)
        carry //= 10

    while carry:
        digits.append(carry % 10)
        carry //= 10

    # Subtract B from A: A - B
    borrow = 0
    b = B
    i = 0
    while b > 0 or borrow:
        if i == len(digits):
            digits.append(0)
        sub = b % 10
        b //= 10
        val = digits[i] - sub - borrow
        if val < 0:
            val += 10
            borrow = 1
        else:
            borrow = 0
        digits[i] = val
        i += 1

    # Remove leading zeros
    while len(digits) > 1 and digits[-1] == 0:
        digits.pop()

    # Divide (A - B) by 9 using long division
    out = bytearray()
    rem = 0
    for d in reversed(digits):
        cur = rem * 10 + d
        out.append(48 + cur // 9)
        rem = cur % 9

    # Skip leading zeros of the quotient
    start = 0
    while start < len(out) - 1 and out[start] == 48:
        start += 1

    sys.stdout.buffer.write(out[start:])
    sys.stdout.buffer.write(b'\n')

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
